\tilde{f}(R):=\mathbb{P}{\left(\nexists x:\,\mathbb{B}_{4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega)\right)}\,.

The stationary ergodic random measure
\tilde{\mu}_{\omega}(\,\cdot\,):=\mathcal{L}{\left(\,\cdot\,\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}_{-4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}\left(\omega\right)\right)}

has positive intensity
\tilde{\lambda}_{0}>p_{\mathfrak{r}}\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\left(\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}\right)^{d}\right|

and it holds
\tilde{\mu}_{\omega}(A)\neq 0
implies the existence
of
\mathbb{B}_{4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}{\left(A\right)}
. Assuming
that
\liminf_{R\to\infty}\tilde{f}>0
there exists for every
R>0

a set
\Omega_{R}\subset\Omega
with
\tilde{\mu}_{\omega}{\left(\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}\right)}=0

for every
\omega\in\Omega_{R}
with
\Omega_{R+1}\subset\Omega_{R}

and

\Omega_{\infty}:=\bigcap_{R>0}\Omega_{R}\quad\text{satisfies}\quad\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\infty})=\liminf_{R\to\infty}\tilde{f}(R)>0\,.

But for almost every
\omega\in\Omega_{\infty}
it holds by the ergodic
theorem

\lim_{R\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}\right|^{-1}\tilde{\mu}_{\omega}{\left(\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}\right)}\geq\lambda_{0}\,,

which implies the existence of
\mathbb{B}_{4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega)
,
a contradiction.
∎

Definition 3.15

(Isotropic cone mixing)
.

A
random set
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega)
is isotropic cone mixing if there exists
a jointly stationary point process
\mathbb{X}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
or
2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\mathfrak{r}>0
, such that almost surely two points
x,y\in\mathbb{X}
have mutual
minimal distance
2\mathfrak{r}
and such that
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\mathbb{X}(\omega)\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega)
.
Further there exists a function
f(R)
with
f(R)\to 0
as
\mathbb{R}\to\infty

and
\alpha\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
such that with
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{E}}:=\left\{e_{1},\dots e_{d}\right\}\cup\left\{-e_{1},\dots-e_{d}\right\}

(
\left\{e_{1},\dots e_{d}\right\}
being the canonical basis of

\mathbb{R}^{d}
)

\mathbb{P}{\left(\forall e\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{E}}:\;\mathbb{X}\cap\mathbb{C}_{e,\alpha,R}(0)\neq\emptyset\right)}\geq 1-f{\left(R\right)}\,.

(3.12)

Lemma 3.16

(A simple sufficient criterion for (
3.12
))
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
be stationary ergodic and
(\mathfrak{r},\tilde{f})
-regular.
Then
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
is isotropic cone mixing with
f(R)=2d\tilde{f}{\left(\left(\left(\tan\alpha\right)^{-1}+1\right)^{-1}R\right)}

and with

\mathbb{X}(\omega):=\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}{\left(\omega\right)}\right)}=2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}_{-\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\omega\right)}=\left\{x\in 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\,:\;\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right\}

(3.13)

from Lemma
2.59
. Vice versa, if
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
is isotropic
cone mixing for
f
then
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
satisfies (
1.6
)
with
\tilde{f}=f
.

Proof of Lemma
3.16
.

Because of
\mathbb{P}{\left(A\cup B\right)}\leq\mathbb{P}{\left(A\right)}+\mathbb{P}{\left(B\right)}
it holds for
a>1

\mathbb{P}{\left(\exists e\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{E}}\,:\;\nexists x\in\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(aRe\right)}:\,\mathbb{B}_{4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(aRe\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\leq 2d\tilde{f}(R)\,.

The existence of
\mathbb{B}_{4\sqrt{d}\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(aRe\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}{\left(\omega\right)}

implies that there exists at least one
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\left(\omega\right)\right)

such that
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(aRe\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}{\left(\omega\right)}

and we find

\mathbb{P}{\left(\exists e\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{E}}\,:\;\nexists x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}:\,\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(x\right)}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(aRe\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\leq 2d\tilde{f}(R)\,.

In particular, for
\alpha=\arctan a^{-1}
and
R
large enough
we discover

\mathbb{P}\left(\exists e\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{E}}\,:\;\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\cap\mathbb{C}_{e,\alpha,\left(a+1\right)R}\left(0\right)=\emptyset\right)\leq 2d\tilde{f}(R)\,.

The relation (
3.12
) holds with
f(R)=2d\tilde{f}{\left(\left(a+1\right)^{-1}R\right)}
.

The other direction is evident.
∎

Properties of
\mathbb{X}

The formulation of Definition
3.15
is particularly
useful for the following statement.

Lemma 3.17

(Size distribution of cells)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega)
be a stationary
and ergodic random open set that is isotropic cone mixing for
\mathbb{X}(\omega)
,

\mathfrak{r}>0
,
f:\,(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
and
\alpha\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
.
Then
\mathbb{X}
and its Voronoi tessellation have the following properties:

1.

If
G(x)
is the open Voronoi cell of
x\in\mathbb{X}(\omega)
with diameter

d{\left(x\right)}
then
d
is jointly stationary with
\mathbb{X}
and for some
constant
C_{\alpha}>0
depending only on
\alpha

\mathbb{P}(d(x)>D)<f{\left(C_{\alpha}^{-1}\frac{D}{2}\right)}\,.

(3.14)

2.

For
x\in\mathbb{X}(\omega)
let
\mathcal{I}{\left(x\right)}:=\left\{y\in\mathbb{X}\,:\;G{\left(y\right)}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G{\left(x\right)}\right)}\not=\emptyset\right\}
.
Then

\#\mathcal{I}{\left(x\right)}\leq\left(\frac{4d{\left(x\right)}}{\mathfrak{r}}\right)^{d}\,.

(3.15)

Proof.

1. W.l.o.g. let
x_{k}=0
. The first part follows from the definition
of isotropic cone mixing: We take arbitrary points
x_{\pm j}\in C_{\pm\mathrm{e}_{j},\alpha,R}(0)\cap\mathbb{X}
.
Then the planes given by the respective equations
\left(x-\frac{1}{2}x_{\pm j}\right)\cdot x_{\pm j}=0

define a bounded cell around
0
, with a maximal diameter
D(\alpha,R)=2C_{\alpha}R

which is proportional to
R
. The constant
C_{\alpha}
depends
nonlinearly on
\alpha
with
C_{\alpha}\to\infty
as
\alpha\to\frac{\pi}{2}
.
Estimate (
3.14
) can now be concluded from
the relation between
R
and
D(\alpha,R)
and from (
3.12
).

2. This follows from Lemma
2.30
.
∎

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}
be a stationary and ergodic
random point process with minimal mutual distance
2\mathfrak{r}
for
\mathfrak{r}>0

and let
f:\,(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
be such that the Voronoi tessellation
of
\mathbb{X}
has the property

\forall x\in\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\,:\quad\mathbb{P}(d(x)>D)=f{\left(D\right)}\,.

Furthermore, let
n,s:\,\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}\to[1,\infty)
be measurable and
i.i.d. among
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}
and let
n,s,d
be independent from each
other. Let either

G_{n(x)}{\left(x\right)}=\begin{cases}x+n(x)\left(G{\left(x\right)}-x\right)&\text{or }\\
\mathbb{B}_{n(x)d(x)}{\left(x\right)}\end{cases}

be the cell
G{\left(x\right)}
enlarged by the factor
n(x)
or a ball of
radius
n(x)d(x)
arround
x
, let
d(x)=\mathrm{diam}G(x)
and let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{n}{\left(y\right)}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\chi_{G_{n}{\left(x\right)}}d(x)^{\eta}s(x)^{\xi}n(x)^{\zeta}\,,

where
\eta,\xi,\zeta>0
are fixed a constant. Then
\mathfrak{b}_{n}
is
jointly stationary with
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}
and for every
r>1
there exists

C\in(0,+\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{\left(\mathfrak{b}_{n}^{p}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\left(k+1\right)^{d\left(p+1\right)+\eta p+r\left(p-1\right)}\left(S+1\right)^{\xi p+r\left(p-1\right)}\left(N+1\right)^{d\left(p+1\right)+\zeta p+r\left(p-1\right)}\mathbb{P}_{d,k}\mathbb{P}_{n,N}\mathbb{P}_{s,S}\right)\,.

(3.16)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{d,k}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}{\left(d(x)\in[k,k+1)\right)}=f{\left(k\right)}-f{\left(k+1\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n,N}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}{\left(n(x)\in[N,N+1)\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{s,S}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}{\left(s(x)\in[S,S+1)\right)}\,.

Corollary 3.19
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
3.18

let additionally
n=const
,
s=const
. Then

\mathbb{E}{\left(\mathfrak{b}^{p}\right)}\leq C\sum_{k,N=1}^{\infty}\left(k+1\right)^{d+(d+\eta+1)p}f(k)\,.

Proof of Lemma
3.18
.

We write
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}=\left(x_{i}\right)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
,
d_{i}=d(x_{i})
,

n_{i}=n(x_{i})
,
s_{i}:=s(x_{i})
. Let

\displaystyle X_{k,N,S}(\omega)

\displaystyle:=\left\{x_{i}\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}\,:\;d_{i}\in[k,k+1),\,n_{i}\in[N,N+1),\,s_{i}\in[S,S+1)\right\}\,,

\displaystyle A_{k,N,S}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{x\in X_{k,N,S}}G_{n(x)}{\left(x\right)}\,,\qquad A_{k,N}:=\bigcup_{S\in\mathbb{N}}A_{k,N,S}\,,\qquad X_{k,N}:=\bigcup_{S\in\mathbb{N}}X_{k,N,S}\,.

We observe that the mutual minimal distance implies

\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\quad\#\left\{x_{i}\in X_{k,N,S}:\,x\in G_{n(x_{i})}{\left(x_{i}\right)}\right\}\leq\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\left(N+1\right)^{d}\,\left(k+1\right)^{d}\mathfrak{r}^{-d}\,,

(3.17)

which follows from the uniform boundedness of cells
G_{n(x)}{\left(x\right)}
,

x\in X_{k,N}
and the minimal distance of
\left|x_{i}-x_{j}\right|>2\mathfrak{r}
.
Then, writing
B_{R}:=\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}
for every
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
it holds by
stationarity and the ergodic theorem

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{\left(y\in G_{n_{i}}{\left(x_{i}\right)}\,:\;x_{i}\in X_{k,N,S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\lim_{R\to\infty}\left|B_{R}\right|^{-1}\left|A_{k,N}\cap B_{R}\right|\mathbb{P}_{s,S}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{R\to\infty}\left|B_{R}\right|^{-1}\left|B_{R}\cap\bigcup_{x_{i}\in X_{k,N}}G_{n_{i}}{\left(x_{i}\right)}\right|\mathbb{P}_{s,S}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{R\to\infty}\left|B_{R}\right|^{-1}\sum_{x_{i}\in X_{k,N}\cap B_{R}}\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|\left(N+1\right)^{d}\left(k+1\right)^{d}\mathfrak{r}^{-d}\mathbb{P}_{s,S}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{P}_{d,k}\mathbb{P}_{n,N}\mathbb{P}_{s,S}\left(N+1\right)^{d}\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|\left(k+1\right)^{d}\mathfrak{r}^{-d}\,.

In the last inequality we made use of the fact that every cell
G_{n(x)}(x)
,

x\in X_{k,N}
, has volume smaller than
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\left(N+1\right)^{d}\left(k+1\right)^{d}
.
We note that for
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\chi_{G_{n}{\left(x\right)}}d(x)^{\eta}s(x)^{\xi}n(x)^{\zeta}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{S=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{x\in X_{k,N,S}}\chi_{G_{n(x)}{\left(x\right)}}\left(k+1\right)^{\eta}(N+1)^{\xi}(S+1)^{\zeta}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left(\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{k,N,S}^{q}\right)^{\frac{p}{q}}\left(\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{k,N,S}^{-p}\left(\sum_{x\in X_{k,N,S}}\chi_{G_{n(x)}{\left(x\right)}}\left(k+1\right)^{\eta}(N+1)^{\xi}(S+1)^{\zeta}\right)^{p}\right)\,.

Due to (
3.17
) we find

\sum_{x\in X_{k,N,S}}\chi_{G_{n(x)}{\left(x\right)}}\leq\chi_{A_{k,N,S}}\left(N+1\right)^{d}\left(k+1\right)^{d}\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|

and obtain for
q=\frac{p}{p-1}
and
C_{q}:=\left(\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{k,N,S}^{q}\right)^{\frac{p}{q}}\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|^{p}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|B_{R}\right|}\int_{B_{R}}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\chi_{G_{n}{\left(x\right)}}d(x)^{\eta}s(x)^{\xi}n(x)^{\zeta}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{q}\frac{1}{\left|B_{R}\right|}\int_{B_{R}}\left(\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{k,N,S}^{-p}\chi_{A_{k,N,S}}\left(N+1\right)^{dp+\zeta p}\left(k+1\right)^{dp+\eta p}(S+1)^{\xi p}\right)

\displaystyle\to C_{q}\left(\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{k,N,S}^{-p}\left(k+1\right)^{d\left(p+1\right)+\eta p}\left(N+1\right)^{d\left(p+1\right)+\zeta p}(S+1)^{\xi p}\mathbb{P}_{s,S}\mathbb{P}_{d,k}\mathbb{P}_{n,N}\right)

For the sum
\sum_{k,N,S=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{k,N,S}^{q}
to converge,
it is sufficient that
\alpha_{k,N,S}^{q}=\left(k+1\right)^{-r}\left(N+1\right)^{-r}\left(S+1\right)^{-r}

for some
r>1
. Hence, for such
r
it holds
\alpha_{k,N,S}=\left(k+1\right)^{-r/q}\left(N+1\right)^{-r/q}\left(S+1\right)^{-r/q}

and thus (
3.16
).
∎

4
Extension and Trace Properties
from
\left(\delta,M\right)
-Regularity

Figure 3:
Gray: a Poisson ball process. Black balls:
balls of radius
\mathfrak{r}>0
. Red Balls: radius
\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}
.
The Voronoi tessellation is generated from the centers of the red
balls. The existence of such tessellations is discussed in Section

3.2
. Blue region:
\mathfrak{A}_{1,k}
.

4.1
Preliminaries

For this whole section, let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
be a Lipschitz domain which furthermore
satisfies the following assumption.

Remark 4.1
.

All calculations that follow in the present Section
4

equally work for arbitrarily distributed radii
\mathfrak{r}_{a}
associated
to
x_{a}
and replacing the constant
\mathfrak{r}
, e.g. with

\mathcal{M}_{a}u:=\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}_{a}}{16}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}}u\,,\qquad\overline{\nabla^{\bot}_{\mathcal{M},a}}u:=\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}_{a}}{16}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}}\left(\nabla-\nabla^{s}\right)u\,.

However, for simplicity of presentation, we chose to work with constant

\mathfrak{r}
from the start.

Assumption 4.2
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
be an open (unbounded)
set and let
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}=\left(x_{a}\right)_{a\in\mathbb{N}}
be a set of points
having mutual distance
\left|x_{a}-x_{b}\right|>2\mathfrak{r}
if
a\neq b

and with
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
for every
a\in\mathbb{N}

(e.g.
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}})
, see (
2.51
)). We construct from

\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}
a Voronoi tessellation and denote by
G_{a}:=G(x_{a})

the Voronoi cell corresponding to
x_{a}
with diameter
d_{a}

with
\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}:=\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};[0,1])

be monotone decreasing with
\tilde{\Phi}_{0}^{\prime}>-\frac{4}{\mathfrak{r}}
,
\tilde{\Phi}_{0}(x)=1

if
x\leq 0
and
\tilde{\Phi}_{0}(x)=0
for
x\geq\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}
.
We define on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
the Lipschitz functions

\tilde{\Phi}_{a}(x):=\tilde{\Phi}_{0}\left(\mathrm{dist}\left(x,G_{a}\right)\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi_{a}(x):=\tilde{\Phi}_{a}(x)\left(\sum_{b}\tilde{\Phi}_{b}(x)\right)^{-1}\,.

(4.1)

Lemma
2.20
implies

\forall x\in\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}\,:\quad\#\left\{b\,:\;x\in\mathfrak{A}_{1,b}\right\}\leq\left(\frac{4d_{a}}{\mathfrak{r}}\right)^{d}

(4.2)

and thus (
4.1
) yields for some
C
depending only
on
\tilde{\Phi}_{0}
that

\left|\nabla\Phi_{a}\right|\leq Cd_{a}^{d}\quad\text{and}\quad\forall k:\,\left|\nabla\Phi_{k}\right|\chi_{\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}}\leq Cd_{a}^{d}\,.

(4.3)

Definition 4.3

(Weak Neighbors)
.

Under
the Assumption
4.2
, two points
x_{a},x_{b}\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}

are called to be weakly connected (or weak neighbors), written
a\sim\sim b

or
x_{a}\sim\sim x_{b}
if
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(G_{b}\right)}\neq\emptyset
.
For
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open we say
\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}\sim\sim\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
if
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\neq\emptyset
.
We then define

\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}):=\left\{x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}:\;\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}\sim\sim\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\neq\emptyset\right\}\,,\quad\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\sim\sim}:=\bigcup_{\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}\sim\sim\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}\,.

(4.4)

In view of Assumption
4.2
we bound
\delta_{\Delta}

by
\mathfrak{r}>0
and recall (
3.1
).
As announced in the introduction, we apply Corollary
3.7

for
n\in\mathbb{N}
(we study mostly
n=1
and
n=2
in the following)
to obtain a complete covering of
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
by balls
\mathbb{B}_{\tilde{\rho}_{n}\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}
,

\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, where
\tilde{\rho}_{n}{\left(p\right)}:=2^{-5}\rho_{n}{\left(p\right)}
.
Recalling (
3.2
)–(
3.3
) we
define with
\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}:=\tilde{\rho}_{n}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}
,

\hat{\rho}_{n,i}:=\hat{\rho}_{n,3}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}

and

A_{1,i}^{n}:=\mathbb{B}_{\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}\,,\quad A_{2,i}^{n}:=\mathbb{B}_{3\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}\,,\quad A_{3,i}^{n}:=\mathbb{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}\,,\quad B_{n,i}:=\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{8}\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}\,,

(4.5)

where we recall the construction of
\mathfrak{r}_{n,\alpha,i}
and
y_{n,\alpha,i}

in (
1.16
)–(
1.17
) and note that

\mathbb{B}_{\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}\supset\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{n,\alpha,i}}{\left(y_{n,\alpha,i}\right)}

independent from
\alpha
.

Lemma 4.4
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in[0,1]

and any two balls
A_{1,i}^{n}\cap A_{1,j}^{n}\neq\emptyset
either

A_{1,i}^{n}\subset A_{2,j}^{n}
or
A_{1,j}^{n}\subset A_{2,i}^{n}

and

A_{1,i}^{n}\cap A_{1,j}^{n}\neq\emptyset\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}\right)}\subset A_{2,j}^{n}\text{ and }\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\rho}_{n,j}}{\left(p_{j}\right)}\subset A_{2,i}^{n}\,.

(4.6)

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C
depending only on the dimension

d
and some
\hat{d}\in[0,d]
such that

\displaystyle\forall k\quad

\displaystyle\#\left\{j\,:\;A_{1,j}^{n}\cap A_{1,i}^{n}\neq\emptyset\right\}+\#\left\{j\,:\;A_{2,j}^{n}\cap A_{2,i}^{n}\neq\emptyset\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\,,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\forall x\quad

\displaystyle\#\left\{j\,:\;x\in A_{1,j}^{n}\right\}+\#\left\{j\,:\;x\in A_{2,j}^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C+1\,,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\forall x\quad

\displaystyle\#\left\{j:\,x\in\overline{\mathbb{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{n,j}}{\left(p_{j}\right)}}\right\}

\displaystyle<C(1+M_{[\frac{3\delta}{8},\frac{\delta}{8}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}(x))^{n\hat{d}}\,.

(4.9)

Finally, there exist non-negative functions
\phi_{n,0}
and
\left(\phi_{n,i}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

independent from
\alpha
such that for
k\geq 1
:
\mathrm{supp}\phi_{n,i}\subset A_{1,i}^{n}
,

\phi_{n,i}|_{B_{n,j}}\equiv 0
for
k\neq j
. Further,
\phi_{n,0}\equiv 0

on all
B_{n,i}
and on
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\phi_{n,i}\equiv 1

and there exists
C
depending only on
d
such that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

it holds

x\in A_{1,i}^{n}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\forall j\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}:\quad\left|\nabla\phi_{n,j}(x)\right|\leq C\tilde{\rho}_{n,i}^{-1}\,.

(4.10)

Remark 4.5
.

We usually can improve

\hat{d}
to at least
\hat{d}=d-1
. To see this assume
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}

is flat on the scale of
\delta
. Then all points
p_{i}
lie on
a
d-1
-dimensional plane and we can thus improve the argument in
the following proof to
\hat{d}=d-1
.

Proof.

(
4.6
) follows from (
3.5
)
2
.
For improved readability we drop the indeces
n
and
\alpha
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
be fixed. By construction in Corollary
3.7
,
every
A_{1,j}
with
A_{1,j}\cap A_{1,k}\neq\emptyset
satisfies

\tilde{\rho}_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\rho}_{k}
and hence if
A_{1,j}\cap A_{1,k}\neq\emptyset

and
A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,k}\neq\emptyset
we find
\left|p_{j}-p_{i}\right|\geq\frac{1}{4}\tilde{\rho}_{k}

and
\left|p_{j}-p_{k}\right|\leq 3\tilde{\rho}_{k}
. This implies
(
4.7
)–(
4.8
)
for
A_{1,j}
and the statement for
A_{2,j}
follows analogously.

For two points
p_{i}
,
p_{j}
such that
x\in A_{3,i}\cap A_{3,j}

it holds due to the triangle inequality
\left|p_{i}-p_{j}\right|\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{4}\hat{\rho}_{i},\frac{1}{4}\hat{\rho}_{j}\right\}
.
Let
\mathbb{X}(x):=\left\{p_{i}\in\mathbb{X}:\,x\in\overline{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{8}\hat{\rho}_{i}}{\left(p_{i}\right)}}\right\}

and choose
\tilde{p}(x)=\tilde{p}\in\mathbb{X}(x)
such that
\delta_{\text{m}}:=\delta{\left(\tilde{p}\right)}

is maximal. Then
\mathbb{X}(x)\subset\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{4}\delta_{\text{m}}}{\left(\tilde{p}\right)}

and every
p_{i}\in\mathbb{X}(x)
satisfies
\delta_{\text{m}}>\delta_{i}>\frac{1}{3}\delta_{\text{m}}
.
Correspondingly,
\tilde{\rho}_{i}>\frac{1}{3}\delta_{\text{m}}2^{-5}\tilde{M}_{\frac{\delta_{i}}{8}}^{-n}>\frac{1}{3}\delta_{\text{m}}2^{-5}\tilde{M}_{\frac{3\delta_{m}}{8}}^{-n}

for all such
p_{i}
. In view of (
3.5
)
this lower local bound of
\tilde{\rho}_{i}
implies a lower local
bound on the mutual distance of the
p_{i}
. Since this distance
is proportional to
\delta_{\text{m}}\tilde{M}_{\frac{3\delta_{m}}{8}}^{-n}
,
this implies (
4.9
) with

\hat{d}=d
. This is by the same time the upper estimate on
\hat{d}
.

Let
\phi:\,\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be symmetric, smooth, monotone on
(0,\infty)

with
\phi^{\prime}\leq 2
and
\phi=0
on
(1,\infty)
. For each
k
we
consider a radially symmetric smooth function
\hat{\phi}_{k}{\left(x\right)}:=\phi{\left(\frac{\left|x-p_{k}\right|^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}_{k}}\right)}

and an additional function
\tilde{\phi}_{0}\left(x\right)=\mathrm{dist}{\left(\,x,\,\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cup\bigcup_{k}B_{n,k}\,\right)}
.
In a similar way we may modify
\tilde{\phi}_{k}:=\hat{\phi}_{k}\,\mathrm{dist}{\left(x,\bigcup_{j\neq k}B_{n,j}\right)}

such that
\tilde{\phi}_{k}|_{B_{n,j}}\equiv 0
for
j\neq k
. Then
we define
\phi_{k}:=\tilde{\phi}/\left(\tilde{\phi}_{0}+\sum_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{j}\right)
.
Note that by construction of
\mathfrak{r}_{k}
and
y_{k}
we find
\phi_{k}|_{B_{k}}\equiv 1

and
\sum_{k\geq 1}\phi_{k}\equiv 1
on
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
.

Estimate (
4.10
) follows from (
4.7
).
∎

4.2
Extensions preserving the
Gradient norm via
\left(\delta,M\right)
-Regularity of
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}

By Lemma
2.3
in case
n=1
there exist
local extension operator

\mathcal{U}_{n,i}:\,\,W^{1,p}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap A_{3,i}^{n}\right)}\,\to\,W^{1,p}{\left(\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{8}\rho_{n,i}}{\left(p_{i}^{n}\right)}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\,\hookrightarrow\,W^{1,p}{\left(A_{2,i}^{n}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}

(4.11)

which is linear continuous with bounds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\mathcal{U}_{n,i}u\right\|_{L^{p}(A_{2,i}^{n}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}})}

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{n,i}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{p}\left(A_{3,i}^{n}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\,,

(4.12)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{U}_{n,i}u\right\|_{L^{p}(A_{2,i}^{1}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|u\right\|_{L^{p}\left(A_{3,i}^{1}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\,.

(4.13)

Of course, higher
n>1
are always valid, but the result becomes
worse, as we will see. However, in case
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
is locally
always in the upper half plane, the case
n=0
is also valid, improving
the estimates of the extension operators significantly. This phenomenon
is acknowledged through the Definition
1.9

of the extension order.

Definition 4.6
.

Using Notation
1.10
for every

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}:\,C^{1}{\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}}\right)}

\displaystyle\to C^{1}{\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\right)}\,,

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\chi_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{i\neq 0}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left(\phi_{n,i}\left(\mathcal{U}_{n,i}{\left(u-\tau_{n,\alpha,i}u\right)}+\tau_{n,\alpha,i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)+\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\,.

(4.14)

Due to the defintions, we find

\tau_{n,\alpha,i}\mathcal{M}_{a}u=\mathcal{M}_{a}u\,.

(4.15)

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}

be a Lipschitz domain (i.e. locally
\left(\delta,M\right)
-regular)
with
\delta_{\Delta}
bounded by
\mathfrak{r}>0
and let Assumption
1.8

hold and let
\hat{d}
be the constant from (
4.9
).
Then for every bounded open
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\mathbb{B}_{10\mathfrak{r}}{\left(0\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}

and
1\leq r<p
the linear operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}:\,W^{1,p}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\to W^{1,r}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)

is continuous and writing

f_{\alpha,n,\hat{d}}(M,\cdot\,):=\left(\left(1+M_{[\frac{3\delta}{8},\frac{\delta}{8}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{n\hat{d}}\left(1+M_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{r}\left(1+M_{[\tilde{\rho}_{n}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\alpha(d-1)}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-r}}

the operator
\mathcal{U}_{n,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfies for some
C
not depending
on
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\nabla\left(\mathcal{U}_{n,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}u\right)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}}f_{\alpha,n,\hat{d}}(M)\right)^{r\frac{p-r}{p}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\rho_{1,i}^{-1}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{n,\alpha,i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\right|^{r}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{a:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}>0}\sum_{b:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{b}<0}\frac{\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{b}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{a}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}u\right)\right|^{r}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\mathcal{U}_{n,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}u\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}(1+M_{[\frac{3\delta}{8},\frac{\delta}{8}],\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{\frac{p\hat{d}}{p-r}}\right)^{\frac{p-r}{p}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}\,,

(4.18)

where

D_{l+}^{\Phi}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\neq 0:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}<0\end{subarray}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}\right|\,.

(4.19)

Remark
.

Since the covering
A_{1,i}
is locally finite we find

\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\rho_{1,i}^{-1}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{n,\alpha,i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\right|^{r}\leq\sum_{i\neq 0}\rho_{1,i}^{-r}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left|\tau_{n,\alpha,i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right|^{r}\,.

4.3
Extensions preserving the Symmetric Gradient norm via
\left(\delta,M\right)
-Regularity
of
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}

By Lemmas
3.4
and
2.7

in case
n=2
the local extension operator

\mathcal{U}_{n,k}:\,\,W^{1,p}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap A_{3,k}^{n}\right)}\,\to\,W^{1,p}{\left(\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{8}\rho_{n,k}}{\left(p_{k}^{n}\right)}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\,\hookrightarrow\,W^{1,p}{\left(A_{2,k}^{n}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}

(4.20)

is linear continuous with bounds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{s}\mathcal{U}_{n,k}u\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{8}\rho_{n,k}}{\left(p_{k}^{n}\right)}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{M}_{n,k}^{2}\left\|\nabla^{s}u\right\|_{L^{p}\left(A_{3,k}^{n}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\right)}\,.

(4.21)

Like in Section
4.2
lower values
of
n
are possible, acknowledged by Definition
1.9

of symmetric extension order.

Definition 4.8
.

Using the notation of Definition
1.13
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}:\,C^{1}{\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}}\right)}

\displaystyle\to C^{1}{\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\right)}\,,

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto\chi_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{k}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left(\phi_{n,k}\left(\mathcal{U}_{n,k}{\left(u-\tau_{n,\alpha,k}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)}+\tau_{n,\alpha,k}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)+\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)

(4.22)

where
\mathcal{U}_{n,k}
are the extension operators on
A_{3,k}^{n}
given
by the symmetric extension order of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
.

By definition we verify
\nabla^{s}\left(u-\tau_{n,\alpha,i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)=\nabla^{s}u

as well as

\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{n,\alpha,i}}{\left(y_{n,\alpha,i}\right)}}\left(\nabla-\nabla^{s}\right)\left(u-\tau_{n,\alpha,i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)=0\,,\qquad\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{n,\alpha,i}}{\left(y_{n,\alpha,i}\right)}}\left(u-\tau_{n,\alpha,i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)=0

and similarly for
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u
. Furthermore, it holds

\tau_{n,\alpha,i}^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u=\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\,.

(4.23)

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}

be a locally
\left(\delta,M\right)
-regular open set with delta
bounded by
\mathfrak{r}>0
and let Assumption
1.8
hold
and let
\hat{d}
be the constant from (
4.9
).
Then for every bounded open
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
1\leq r<p
the operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}:\,W^{1,p}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\to W^{1,r}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)

is linear, well defined and with

f_{\alpha,n,\hat{d}}^{\mathfrak{s}}(M,\cdot\,):=\left(\left(1+M_{[\frac{3\delta}{8},\frac{\delta}{8}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\hat{d}}\left(1+M_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{2r}\left(1+M_{[\tilde{\rho}_{n}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\alpha(d-1)}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-r}}

satisfies

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\nabla^{s}\left(\mathcal{U}_{2,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}u\right)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}}f_{\alpha,n,\hat{d}}^{\mathfrak{s}}(M)\right)^{r\frac{p-r}{p}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\nabla^{s}u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\rho_{1,i}^{-1}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{n,\alpha,i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)\right|^{r}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{a:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}>0}\sum_{b:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{b}<0}\frac{\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{b}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)\right|^{r}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\mathcal{U}_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}u\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}(1+M_{[\frac{3\delta}{8},\frac{\delta}{8}],\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{\frac{2p\hat{d}}{p-r}}\right)^{\frac{p-r}{p}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}\,,

(4.26)

where
D_{l+}^{\Phi}
is given by (
4.19
)

4.4
Support

Theorem 4.10
.

For both operators given in (
4.14
)
and (
4.22
) the following holds: For every
bounded open set
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
with
0\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
and
n_{0},n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
let

\forall M>1:\qquad\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{M}:=\bigcup_{x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{r}}\cap M\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}\,.

If the mesoscopic regularity function
\tilde{f}
of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
satisfies

\tilde{f}(D)\leq CD^{-\frac{d-1}{\alpha}+\beta}
for some
C>0
,

\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta>1
then there exists almost surely
M_{0}>1

such that for every
M>M_{0}
it holds
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{M}\subset\mathbb{B}_{M^{\alpha}}{\left(M\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}
.

Proof.

We consider two balls
\mathbb{B}_{r}{\left(0\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}
with
r>0
.

We write
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}:=M\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
and
\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}:=\mathbb{B}_{M^{\alpha}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\right)}

for
\alpha\in(0,1)
with
\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{\complement}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}
.
For
k\in\mathbb{N}
we introduce

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,k}:=\left\{x\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}:\,\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M})\in[k,k)\right\}

and find

\mathbb{P}{\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{M}\subset\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\right)}=1-\sum_{k}\mathbb{P}{\left(\exists x_{a}\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,k}\cap\mathbb{X}_{r}:\,\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}\cap\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{\complement}\neq\emptyset\right)}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{\left(\exists x_{a}\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,k}\cap\mathbb{X}_{r}:\,\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}\cap\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{\complement}\neq\emptyset\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}{\left(\exists x_{a}\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,k}\cap\mathbb{X}_{r}:\,\mathbb{B}_{2d_{a}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}\cap\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{\complement}\neq\emptyset\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\mathbb{P}{\left(d_{a}>\frac{k}{2}+M^{\alpha}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq CM^{d-1}\left(\frac{k}{2}+M^{\alpha}\right)^{-\left(\frac{d-1}{\alpha}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\right)}\leq CM^{-\beta_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)^{-\beta_{2}}

where
C
depends only on the minimal mutual distance of the points,
i.e.
\mathfrak{r}
, and the shape of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
. Now, since
\beta>1
we can
choose
\beta_{2}>1
and find

\mathbb{P}{\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{M}\subset\mathbb{B}_{M,\alpha,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\right)}\geq 1-CM^{-\beta_{1}}\,.

Since the right hand side converges to
1
as
M\to\infty
, we can
conclude.
∎

4.5
Proof of Lemmas
4.7
and
4.9

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\alpha_{i}
,
u_{i}
,
i=1\dots n
,
be a family of real numbers such that
\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}=0
and
let
\alpha_{+}:=\sum_{i:\,\alpha_{i}>0}\alpha_{i}
. Then

\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}u_{i}=\sum_{i:\,\alpha_{i}>0}\sum_{j:\,\alpha_{j}<0}\frac{\alpha_{i}\left|\alpha_{j}\right|}{\alpha_{+}}\left(u_{i}-u_{j}\right)\,.

Proof.

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}u_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\,\alpha_{i}>0}\alpha_{i}u_{i}+\sum_{j:\,\alpha_{j}<0}\alpha_{j}u_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\,\alpha_{i}>0}\alpha_{i}\sum_{j:\,\alpha_{j}<0}\frac{-\alpha_{j}}{\alpha_{+}}u_{i}+\sum_{j:\,\alpha_{j}<0}\alpha_{j}\sum_{i:\,\alpha_{i}>0}\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\alpha_{+}}u_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:\,\alpha_{i}>0}\sum_{j:\,\alpha_{j}<0}\frac{\alpha_{i}\left|\alpha_{j}\right|}{\alpha_{+}}\left(u_{i}-u_{j}\right)\,.

∎

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

For improved readability, we drop the indeces
n
and
\alpha

in the following.

We prove Lemma
4.7
, i.e. (
4.16
)
as (
4.18
) can be
derived in a similar but shorter way. Lemma
4.9

can be proved in a similar way with some inequalities used below being
replaced by the “symmetrized” counterparts. We will make some
comments towards this direction in Step 4 of this proof.

For shortness of notation (and by abuse of notation) we write

\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}g:=\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}g\,,\qquad\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}g:=\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}g

and similar for integrals over
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}

and
\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
. For simplicity of notation,
we further drop the index
1
in the subsequent calculations.

We introduce the quantities

\tilde{M}_{\tilde{\rho},i}:=M_{\tilde{\rho}(p_{i})}(p_{i})\,,\qquad\tilde{M}_{\delta,1,i}:=M_{\frac{1}{8}\delta(p_{i})}(p_{i})\,,\qquad\tilde{M}_{\delta,2,i}:=M_{\frac{3}{8}\delta(p_{i})}(p_{i})

note that
\tilde{\rho}_{i}\leq\frac{1}{8}\delta_{i}
as well as

\sqrt{4M_{i}^{2}+2}\leq 2\tilde{M}_{i}
. Writing

\displaystyle u_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{U}_{i}\left(u-\tau_{i}u\right)+\tau_{i}u

\displaystyle\text{on }A_{2,i}

\displaystyle u_{i,a}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{U}_{i}\left(u-\tau_{i}u\right)+\tau_{i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u

\displaystyle\text{on }A_{2,i}\cap\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}

on
A_{2,i}
, The integral over
\nabla\left(\mathcal{U}_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}u\right)

can be estimated via

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\nabla\left(\mathcal{U}_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}u\right)\right|^{r}\leq C_{r}\left(I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}\right)

(4.27)

\displaystyle I_{1}:=\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\phi_{i}\nabla u_{i,a}\right|^{r}\,,\qquad I_{2}:=\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\sum_{a}u_{i,a}\Phi_{a}\nabla\phi_{i}\right|^{r}\,,

\displaystyle I_{3}:=\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\sum_{a}u_{i,a}\phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{a}\right|^{r}\,.

(4.28)

Step 1:
Using (
1.14
) and
\nabla u_{i,a}=\nabla u_{i}

as well as
\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}=1
we conclude

\displaystyle I_{1}=\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\phi_{i}\nabla u_{i}\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{i\neq 0}\phi_{i}\left|\nabla u_{i}\right|^{r}\leq\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{i\neq 0}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left|\nabla u_{i}\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\sum_{i\neq 0}\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\chi_{A_{2,i}}\left|\nabla u_{i}\right|^{r}\leq C\,\sum_{i\neq 0}\tilde{M}_{\delta,2,i}^{r}\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\chi_{A_{3,i}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{r}\,.

It only remains to estimate
\sum_{i}\chi_{A_{3,i}}(x)
. After a
Hölder estimate and using
\tilde{M}_{\delta,2,i}\leq 1+M_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}

on
A_{3,i}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq 0}\tilde{M}_{\delta,2,i}^{r}

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\chi_{A_{3,i}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{r}\leq\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{i\neq 0}\chi_{A_{3,i}}\left(1+M_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{r}\left|\nabla u\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left(\sum_{i\neq 0}\chi_{A_{3,i}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-r}}\left(1+M_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\frac{rp}{p-r}}\right)^{\frac{p-r}{p}}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}\,.

(4.29)

Step 2
: Concerning
I_{2}
, we first observe that for each

j\neq 0
it holds

\chi_{A_{1,j}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}u_{j,a}\nabla\phi_{0}+\chi_{A_{1,j}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{i\neq 0}u_{j,a}\nabla\phi_{i}=0\,.

(4.30)

We use
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\geq\chi_{A_{1,i}}
for every

i\in\mathbb{N}
together with (
4.30
) and (
4.7
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}u_{i,a}\nabla\phi_{i}\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{j\neq 0}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\sum_{i\neq 0}\left(u_{i,a}-u_{j,a}\right)\nabla\phi_{i}+u_{j,a}\nabla\phi_{0}\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:lem:properties-local-rho-convering-2}}}{{\leq}}C\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\Phi_{a}\left(\sum_{j\neq 0}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\sum_{i:\,A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}\neq\emptyset}\left|u_{i,a}-u_{j,a}\right|^{r}\left|\nabla\phi_{i}\right|^{r}+\left|\sum_{j\neq 0}\chi_{A_{1,j}}u_{j,a}\nabla\phi_{0}\right|^{r}\right)\,.

Note that

\forall a,b,i,j:\qquad u_{i,a}-u_{j,a}=u_{i,b}-u_{j,b}=u_{i}-u_{j}\,.

(4.31)

Furthermore
u_{i}
and
u_{j}
are defined on
A_{2,i}
and
A_{2,j}

respectively and
u_{i}=u_{j}
on
\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{j}}{\left(p_{j}\right)}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{i}}{\left(p_{i}\right)}

because of (
4.6
). Furthermore,
both functions can be extended from
A_{2,i}
and
A_{2,j}
to
\tilde{u}_{i}

and
\tilde{u}_{j}
on
\mathbb{B}_{4\tilde{\rho}_{i}}{\left(p_{i}\right)}
and
\mathbb{B}_{4\tilde{\rho}_{j}}{\left(p_{j}\right)}

respectively using Lemma
2.1
such that for
some
C
independent from
i,j

k=i,j:\quad\left\|\nabla\tilde{u}_{k}\right\|_{L^{r}{\left(\mathbb{B}_{4\tilde{\rho}_{k}}{\left(p_{k}\right)}\right)}}\leq C\left\|\nabla\tilde{u}_{k}\right\|_{L^{r}{\left(A_{2,k}\right)}}\,.

Since now
\tilde{u}_{i}=\tilde{u}_{j}
on
\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{j}}{\left(p_{j}\right)}

and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}_{i}}{\left(p_{i}\right)}
we chose
k(i,j)
such that for
\tilde{M}_{k(i,j)}=1+\min\left\{M_{\tilde{\rho},i},M_{\tilde{\rho},j}\right\}

and it holds by the Poincaré inequality (
2.13
),
the microscopic regularity
\alpha
and the estimate (
3.4
)

\int_{A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}}\left|u_{i,a}-u_{j,a}\right|^{r}\left|\nabla\phi_{i}\right|^{r}\leq C\rho_{i}^{-r}\int_{A_{1,k(i,j)}}\left|\tilde{u}_{i}-\tilde{u}_{j}\right|^{r}\leq C\tilde{M}_{k(i,j)}^{\alpha(d-1)}\int_{A_{2,k(i,j)}}\left|\nabla\left(\tilde{u}_{i}-\tilde{u}_{j}\right)\right|^{r}\,.

We obtain with microscopic regularity
\alpha
, the finite covering
(
4.8
) and the proportionality
(
3.5
) that

\displaystyle\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\chi_{A_{1,j}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i:\,A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}\neq\emptyset}\left|u_{i,a}-u_{j,a}\right|^{r}\left|\nabla\phi_{i}\right|^{r}=\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\sum_{i:\,A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}\neq\emptyset}\left|\tilde{u}_{i}-\tilde{u}_{j}\right|^{r}\left|\nabla\phi_{i}\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\sum_{i:\,A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}\neq\emptyset}\tilde{M}_{k(i,j)}^{\alpha(d-1)}\int_{A_{2,j}}\left|\nabla\left(\tilde{u}_{i}-\tilde{u}_{j}\right)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\sum_{i:\,A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}\neq\emptyset}\tilde{M}_{k(i,j)}^{\alpha(d-1)}\left(\int_{A_{2,i}}\left|\nabla\tilde{u}_{i}\right|^{r}+\int_{A_{2,j}}\left|\nabla\tilde{u}_{j}\right|^{r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\sum_{i:\,A_{1,i}\cap A_{1,j}\neq\emptyset}\left(\int_{A_{3,i}\cup A_{3,j}}\tilde{M}_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{r}\left(1+M_{[\tilde{\rho}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\alpha(d-1)}\left|\nabla u\right|^{r}\right)\,.

Next we estimate from (
4.10
)

\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{j\neq 0}\chi_{A_{1,j}}u_{j,a}\nabla\phi_{0}\right|^{r}\leq C\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left(\sum_{j\neq 0}\rho_{j}^{-r}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\left|\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(u-\tau_{j}u\right)\right|^{r}+\left|\sum_{j\neq 0}\rho_{j}^{-1}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\left(\tau_{j}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\right|^{r}\right)\,.

Using once more Assumption
1.8
and

\nabla\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(u-\tau_{j}u\right)=\nabla\left(\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(u-\tau_{j}u\right)+\tau_{j}u\right)=\nabla u_{j}

(4.32)

and
\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}=1
we infer from (
2.13
)

C\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\sum_{j\neq 0}\rho_{j}^{-r}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\left|\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(u-\tau_{j}u\right)\right|^{r}\leq\frac{C}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\sum_{j\neq 0}\left(1+M_{\tilde{\rho},j}\right)^{\alpha(d-1)}\int_{A_{2,j}}\left|\nabla u_{j}\right|^{r}\,.

Now we make use of the extension estimate (
1.14
)
to find

\int_{A_{2,j}}\left|\nabla u_{j}\right|^{r}\leq CM_{\delta,1,j}^{r}\int_{A_{3,j}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{r}

which in total implies for
f_{12}(M)=\left(1+M_{[\frac{1}{8}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\frac{rp}{p-r}}\left(1+M_{[\tilde{\rho}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\frac{p\alpha(d-1)}{p-r}}

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left(\sum_{i\neq 0}\chi_{A_{3,i}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-r}}f_{12}(M)\right)^{\frac{p-r}{p}}\left(\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{j\neq 0}\rho_{j}^{-1}\chi_{A_{1,j}}\left(\tau_{j}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\right|^{r}\,.

Making use of (
4.9
) we
find

\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\chi_{A_{3,i}}\right|\leq\left(1+M_{[\frac{3\delta}{8},\frac{\delta}{8}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right)^{\hat{d}}\,,

and it only remains to estimate
I_{3}
.

Step 3: We observe with help of
\sum_{a}\nabla\Phi_{a}=0
and
\sum_{i\neq 0}\phi_{i}=\phi_{0}

that

\sum_{i\neq 0}\sum_{a}u_{i,a}\phi_{i}\nabla\Phi_{a}=\sum_{i\neq 0}u_{i}\phi_{i}\sum_{a}\nabla\Phi_{a}+\sum_{a}\mathcal{M}_{a}u\nabla\Phi_{a}=\sum_{a}\mathcal{M}_{a}u\nabla\Phi_{a}\,.

and Lemma
4.11
yields

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\phi_{0}\sum_{a}\mathcal{M}_{a}u\nabla\Phi_{a}\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\fint_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{a:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}>0}\sum_{b:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{b}<0}\frac{\partial_{l}\Phi_{a}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{b}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{a}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}u\right)\right|^{r}\,.

Step 4: Concerning the proof of Lemma
4.9

we follow the above lines with the following modifications.

We use the Nitsche extension operators. Hence, instead of (
1.14
)
we use (
1.15
). The local extended functions
are called

\displaystyle u_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{U}_{i}\left(u-\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)+\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u

\displaystyle\text{on }A_{2,i}

\displaystyle u_{i,a}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{U}_{i}\left(u-\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)+\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u

\displaystyle\text{on }A_{2,i}\cap\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}

and (
4.31
) remains valid. We find it worth mentioning
that
\nabla^{s}\left(\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)=0
and hence

\nabla^{s}\left(\phi_{i}\Phi_{a}u_{i,a}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla(\phi_{i}\Phi_{a})\otimes u_{i,a}+u_{i,a}\otimes\nabla(\phi_{i}\Phi_{a})\right)+\phi_{i}\Phi_{a}\nabla^{s}\mathcal{U}_{2,i}\left(u-\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)\,.

We furthermore replace Lemma
2.1
by Lemma

2.6
and the Poincaré inequality (
2.13
)
by (
2.23
). Finally we observe that (
4.32
)
is replaced by

\nabla^{s}\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(u-\tau_{j}u\right)=\nabla^{s}\left(\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(u-\tau_{j}u\right)+\tau_{j}u\right)=\nabla^{s}u_{j}

∎

4.6
Traces on
\left(\delta,M\right)
-Regular
Sets, Proof of Theorem
1.7

Proof.

We use the covering of
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
by
B_{i}:=A_{1,i}^{1}
and
set
\tilde{\rho}_{i}:=\tilde{\rho}_{1,i}
,
\hat{\rho}_{i}:=\hat{\rho}_{i,5}(p_{k}^{1})

and write
M_{i}=M_{\hat{\rho}_{i}}(p_{k}^{1})
,
\hat{B}_{i}:=\mathbb{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{i}}{\left(p_{k}^{1}\right)}
.
Due to Lemma
2.5
we find locally

\left\|\mathcal{T}u\right\|_{L^{p_{0}}(\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap B_{k})}\leq C_{p_{0},p_{0}}\tilde{\rho}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{p_{0}}}\sqrt{4M_{k}^{2}+2}^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}+1}\left\|u\right\|_{W^{1,p_{0}}\left(\hat{B}_{k}\right)}\,.

(4.33)

We thus obtain

\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\cap\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{k}\phi_{k}\mathcal{T}_{k}u\right|^{r}\\
\leq\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{k}\chi_{B_{k}}\tilde{\rho}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{p_{0}-r}}\right)^{\frac{p_{0}-r}{p_{0}}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\sum_{k}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\cap\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\chi_{B_{k}}\tilde{\rho}_{k}\left|\mathcal{T}_{k}u\right|^{p_{0}}\right)^{\frac{r}{p_{0}}}

which yields by the uniform local bound of the covering,
\tilde{\eta}

defined in Lemma
3.12
, twice
the application of (
3.10
)
and (
4.33
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\cap\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\left|\sum_{k}\phi_{k}\mathcal{T}_{k}u\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\cap\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\rho_{5,\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{-\frac{1}{p_{0}-r}}\right)^{\frac{p_{0}-r}{p_{0}}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{k}\chi_{\hat{B}_{k}}\sqrt{4M_{k}^{2}+2}^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}+1}\left(\left|\nabla u\right|^{p_{0}}+\left|u\right|^{p_{0}}\right)\right)^{\frac{r}{p_{0}}}\,.

With Hölders inequality and replacing
M_{k}
by
M_{[\frac{1}{32}\delta],\mathbb{R}^{d}}
,
the last estimate leads to (
1.12
).
The second estimate goes analogue since the local covering by
A_{2,k}

is finite.
∎

5
The Issue of Connectedness

Remark 5.1
.

The following Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3
also hold with

\tau_{i}
and
\mathcal{M}_{a}
replaced by
\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}

respectively.

Lemma 5.2
.

Under Assumptions
1.8
,
4.2

let
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be non-negative and have support

\mathrm{supp}f_{j}\supset\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(x_{j}\right)}
and let
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}f_{j}\equiv 1
.
Writing
\mathbb{X}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}):=\left\{x_{j}:\;\mathrm{supp}f_{j}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\neq\emptyset\right\}
,
and

\displaystyle F_{s,\iota}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathfrak{r}}\cap\mathbb{R}^{d}_{3}}\left|\tilde{\rho}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right|^{-\frac{sr}{s-r}}\tilde{M}^{2-\iota}\right)^{\frac{s-r}{s}}

\displaystyle F_{s,\tilde{s},\iota}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right)}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\tilde{M}^{\frac{\left(\iota-2\right)(\tilde{s}-r)}{r(s-\tilde{s})}}\right)^{r\frac{s-\tilde{s}}{\tilde{s}s}}

\displaystyle F_{s}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},u)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\sum_{x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq 0:\,\partial_{l}\phi_{i}\partial_{l}\phi_{0}<0\end{subarray}}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\right|^{s}\right)^{\frac{r}{s}}

\displaystyle F_{s}^{3,\mathfrak{s}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},u)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\sum_{x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq 0:\,\partial_{l}\phi_{i}\partial_{l}\phi_{0}<0\end{subarray}}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)\right|^{s}\right)^{\frac{r}{s}}

for every
l=1,\dots d
and
r<\tilde{s}<s
it holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\rho_{1,i}^{-1}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{n,\alpha,i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}F_{s,2}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\\
F_{s,d}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s,\tilde{s},d}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},u)\end{cases}\,,

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}}\sum_{a}\Phi_{a}\left|\sum_{i\neq 0}\rho_{1,i}^{-r}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left(\tau_{n,\alpha,i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}F_{s,2}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s}^{3,\mathfrak{s}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\\
F_{s,d}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s,\tilde{s},d}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s}^{3,\mathfrak{s}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},u)\end{cases}\,.

Proof.

We find from Hölder’s and Jensen’s inequality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq 0:\,\partial_{l}\phi_{i}\partial_{l}\phi_{0}<0\end{subarray}}\sum_{a}\rho_{1,i}^{-r}\frac{\left|\partial_{l}\phi_{i}\right|}{D_{l+}}\chi_{\mathfrak{A}_{1,a}}\left|\tau_{i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\begin{cases}F_{s,2}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\\
F_{s,d}^{1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s,\tilde{s},d}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\,F_{s}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\end{cases}\,.

The second part follows accordingly.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Under Assumptions
1.8
,
4.2

for every
l=1,\dots d
and
\tilde{\alpha}>0
it holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}u\right)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left(\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}d_{j}^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}s+drs}{s-r}}\chi_{\nabla\Phi_{j}\neq 0}\right)^{\frac{s}{s-r}}\right)^{\frac{s-r}{s}}\cdot\dots

\displaystyle\quad\dots\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\chi_{\nabla\Phi_{j}\neq 0}\frac{d_{j}^{-\tilde{\alpha}\frac{s}{r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{k}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}u\right|^{s}\right)^{\frac{r}{s}}\,,

with the similar formula holding for
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
replaced by

\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}^{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Proof.

We observe with help of (
4.3
) and with
Lemma
3.17
.2)

\displaystyle\forall x:\quad\sup_{k}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|(x)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\left\{\left|\nabla\Phi_{k}(x)\right|\,:\;x\in\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(G_{k}\right)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\left\{d_{k}^{d}\,:\;x\in G_{k}\right\}\,,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(G_{j}\right)}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|(x)

\displaystyle\leq Cd_{j}^{d}\,.

(5.2)

We write

I:=\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left(2-\phi_{0}\right)\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}u\right)\right|^{r}

and find

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|^{r}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{k}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}u\right|^{r}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left(\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{d_{j}^{\alpha\frac{s}{s-r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|^{\frac{sr}{s-r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\right)^{\frac{s-r}{s}}\cdot\dots

\displaystyle\qquad\dots\cdot\left(\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\chi_{\nabla\Phi_{j}\neq 0}\frac{d_{j}^{-\alpha\frac{s}{r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{k}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}u\right|^{s}\right)^{\frac{r}{s}}\,.

Now we make use of (
5.1
) and once more of
Lemma
3.17
.2) to obtain for the first
bracket on the right hand side an estimate of the form

\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|^{\frac{sr}{s-r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|\leq\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|^{\frac{sr}{s-r}-1}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|\leq C\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|d_{j}^{d\frac{sr-s+r}{s-r}}d_{j}^{d}\leq C\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|d_{j}^{d\frac{sr}{s-r}}\,,

which implies

\displaystyle\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{d_{j}^{\alpha\frac{s}{s-r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|^{\frac{sr}{s-r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{d_{j}^{\alpha\frac{s}{s-r}}d_{j}^{\frac{dsr}{s-r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}d_{j}^{\alpha\frac{s}{s-r}}d_{j}^{\frac{dsr}{s-r}}\chi_{\nabla\Phi_{j}\neq 0}\,,

where we used
\sum\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}\right|=D_{l+}^{\Phi}
.
From Hölder’s inequality the Lemma follows.
∎

6
Sample Geometries

6.1
Delaunay Pipes for a Matern Process

For two points
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we denote

P_{r}(x,y):=\left\{y+z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,0\leq z\cdot(x-y)\leq\left|x-y\right|^{2},\,\left|z-z\cdot(x-y)\frac{x-y}{\left|x-y\right|}\right|<r\right\}\,,

the cylinder (or pipe) around the straight line segment connecting

x
and
y
with radius
r>0
.

Recalling Example
2.48
we consider a Poisson
point process
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}(\omega)=\left(x_{i}(\omega)\right)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

with intensity
\lambda
(recall Example
2.48
)
and construct a hard core Matern process
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
by deleting
all points with a mutual distance smaller than
d\mathfrak{r}
for some
\mathfrak{r}>0

(refer to Example
2.49
). From the remaining point process

\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
we construct the Delaunay triangulation
\mathbb{D}(\omega):=\mathbb{D}(X_{\mathrm{mat}}(\omega))

and assign to each
(x,y)\in\mathbb{D}
a random number
\delta(x,y)
in

(0,\mathfrak{r})
in an i.i.d. manner from some probability distribution

\delta(\omega)
. We finally define

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega):=\bigcup_{(x,y)\in\mathbb{D}(\omega)}P_{\delta(x,y)}(x,y)\bigcup_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}}\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{2}}{\left(x\right)}

the family of all pipes generated by the Delaunay grid “smoothed”
by balls with the fix radius
\mathfrak{r}
around each point of the generating
Matern process.

Since the Matern process is mixing and
\delta
is mixing, Lemma

2.34
yields that the whole process is still
ergodic. We start with a trivial observation.

Corollary 6.1
.

The microscopic regularity of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}

is
\alpha=0
(Def.
1.8
) and it holds
\hat{d}=d-1

in Lemma
4.4
. Furthermore both
the extension order and the symmetric extension order are
n=0
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
can be locally represented
as a graph in the upper half space with
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
filling the lower half
space.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

For the Voronoi tessellation
\left(G_{a}\right)_{a\in\mathbb{N}}
corresponding
to
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
holds

\mathbb{P}(d_{a}\geq D)\leq\exp{\left(-\lambda\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|\left(4D\right)^{d}\left(1-e^{-\lambda\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{d}}\right)\right)}\,.

Proof.

For the underlying Poisson point process
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}
it holds for
the void probability inside a ball
\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(x\right)}

\mathbb{P}{\left(\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}(\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(x\right)})=0\right)}=\mathbb{P}_{R,0}:=e^{-\lambda\left|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right|R^{d}}\,.

The probability for a point
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}
to be removed is thus

1-\mathbb{P}_{d\mathfrak{r},0}
and is i.i.d distributed among points of
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}
.
The total probability to not find any point of
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
is thus
given by not finding a point of
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}
plus the probability
that all points of
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{pois}}
are removed, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{\left(\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(x\right)})=0\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda\left|A\right|}\frac{\lambda^{n}\left|A\right|^{n}}{n!}\left(1-\mathbb{P}_{d\mathfrak{r},0}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=\exp{\left(-\lambda\left|A\right|+\lambda\left|A\right|\left(1-\mathbb{P}_{d\mathfrak{r},0}\right)\right)}=e^{-\lambda\left|A\right|\left(1-\mathbb{P}_{d\mathfrak{r},0}\right)}\,.

From here one concludes.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

The family of balls
\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x\right)}
can also be dropped from the model.
However, this would imply we had to remove some of the points from

\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
for the generation of the Voronoi cells. This would cause
technical difficulties which would not change much in the result,
as the probability for the size of Voronoi cells would still decrease
exponentially.

Lemma 6.4
.

\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
is a point process
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}(\omega)
that satisfies Assumption
4.2

and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
is isotropic cone mixing for
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
with exponentially
decreasing
f(R)\leq Ce^{-R^{d}}
and it holds
n=0
and
\alpha=0
.
Furthermore, assume there exists
C_{\delta},a_{\delta}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}(\delta(x,y)<\delta_{0})\leq C_{\delta}e^{-a_{\delta}\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}}
,
then
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{M}>M_{0})\leq Ce^{-aM_{0}}
for some
C,a>0
. If

\mathbb{P}(\delta(x,y)<\delta_{0})\leq C_{\delta}\delta_{0}^{\beta}
then
for every
R\in(0,\infty)
it holds

\mathbb{E}{\left(M_{[\frac{\delta}{2}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{R}\right)}+\mathbb{E}{\left(\tilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{-(\beta+d-1)}\right)}<C\mathbb{E}{\left(\left|x-y\right|\right)},

(6.1)

where
\mathbb{E}{\left(\left|x-y\right|\right)}
is the expectation of the length
of pipes.

Proof.

Isotropic cone mixing:
For
x,y\in 2d\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
the events
\left(x+[0,1]^{d}\right)\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}

and
\left(y+[0,1]^{d}\right)\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
are mutually independent.
Hence

\mathbb{P}{\left(\left(k2dr\,[-1,1]^{d}\right)\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}=\emptyset\right)}\leq\mathbb{P}{\left([-1,1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}=\emptyset\right)}^{k^{d}}\,.

Hence the open set
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
is isotropic cone mixing for
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}

with exponentially decaying
f(R)\leq Ce^{-R^{d}}
.

Estimate on the distribution of
M
:
By definition of the
Delaunay triangulation, two pipes intersect only if they share one
common point
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
.

Given three points
x,y,z\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
with
x\sim y
and
x\sim z
,
the highest local Lipschitz constant on
\partial\left(P_{\delta(x,y)}(x,y)\cup P_{\delta(x,z)}(x,z)\right)

is attained in

\tilde{x}=\arg\max\left\{\left|x-\tilde{x}\right|:\,\tilde{x}\in\partial P_{\delta(x,y)}(x,y)\cap\partial P_{\delta(x,z)}(x,z)\right\}\,.

It is bounded by

\max\left\{\arctan\left(\frac{1}{2}\sphericalangle\left((x,y),(x,z)\right)\right),\,\frac{1}{\delta(x,y)},\,\frac{1}{\delta(x,z)}\right\}\,,

where
\alpha:=\sphericalangle\left((x,y),(x,z)\right)
in the following
denotes the angle between
(x,y)
and
(x,z)
, see Figure
4
.
If
d_{x}
is the diameter of the Voronoi cell of
x
, we show that
a necessary (but not sufficient) condition that the angle
\alpha

can be smaller than some
\alpha_{0}
is given by

d_{x}\geq C\frac{1}{\sin\alpha_{0}}\,,

(6.2)

where
C>0
is a constant depending only on the dimension
d
. Since
for small
\alpha
we find
M\approx\frac{1}{\sin\alpha}
, and since
the distribution for
d_{x}
decays subexponentially, also the distribution
for
M
at the junctions of two pipes decays subexponentially. However,
inside the pipes, we find
\Delta(p)=2\delta(x,y)
and hence
\delta_{\Delta}(p)=\delta(x,y)
.
Due to the cylindric structure, we furthermore find essential boundedness
of
M
. This also implies
\alpha=n=0
inside the pipes. At the
junction of Balls and pipes we find
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
to be in the upper
half of the local plane approximation and hence also here
\alpha=n=0

can be chosen (see also Remarks
2.4
and
2.8
).

Concerning the expectation of
M_{[\frac{\delta}{2}],\mathbb{R}^{d}}
and
\delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
,
we only have to accound for the pipes by the above argumentation since
the other contribution to
M
is exponentially distributed. In particular,
we find for one single pipe
P_{\delta(x,y)}(x,y)
that

\int_{P_{\delta(x,y)}(x,y)}\delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{-\alpha-d+1}\leq C\left|x-y\right|\delta(x,y)^{-\alpha}\,,

and hence (
6.1
) due to the
independence of length and diameter. It thus remains to proof (
6.2
).

Figure 4:
Sketch of the proof of Lemma
6.4

and estimate (
6.2
)
.

Proof of (
6.2
):
Given
an angle
\alpha>0
and
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
we derive a lower bound
for the diameter of
G(x)
such that for two neighbors
y,z
of

x
it can hold
\sphericalangle\left((x,y),(x,z)\right)\leq\alpha
.
With regard to Figure
4
, we assume

\left|x-y\right|\geq\left|x-z\right|
.

Writing
d_{x}:=d(x)
the diameter of
G(x)
and
\tilde{\alpha}=\sphericalangle\left((x,z),(z,y)\right)
,
w.l.o.g let
y=(d_{1}+d_{2},0,\dots,0)
, where
d_{1}+d_{2}<d_{x}

and
d_{1}=\left|y-z\right|\cos\tilde{\alpha}
. Hence we can assume

z=(d_{2},-\left|y-z\right|\sin\tilde{\alpha},0\dots 0)
and in what
follows, we focus on the first two coordinates only. The boundaries
between the cells
x
and
z
and
x
and
y
lie on the planes

h_{xz}(t)=\frac{1}{2}z+t\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left|y-z\right|\sin\tilde{\alpha}\\
d_{2}\end{array}\right)\,,\quad h_{xy}(s)=\frac{1}{2}y+s\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\right)

respectively. The intersection of these planes has the first two coordinates

i_{xyz}:=\left(\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{2},-\frac{1}{2}\left|y-z\right|\sin\tilde{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}}{\left|y-z\right|\sin\tilde{\alpha}}\right)\,.

Using the explicit form of
d_{2}
, the latter point has the distance

\left|i_{xyz}\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left|y-z\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}d_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{d_{2}^{2}\cos^{2}\tilde{\alpha}}{\sin^{2}\tilde{\alpha}}

to the origin
x=0
. Using
\left|y-z\right|\sin\tilde{\alpha}=\left|z\right|\sin\alpha

and
d_{2}=\left|y\right|-\left|z\right|\cos\alpha
we obtain

\left|i_{xyz}\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\left|y-z\right|^{2}\left(1+\frac{\left(\left|y\right|-\left|z\right|\cos\alpha\right)^{2}\cos^{2}\tilde{\alpha}}{\left|z\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha}\right)+\left(\left|y\right|-\left|z\right|\cos\alpha\right)^{2}\right)\,.

Given
y
, the latter expression becomes small for
\left|y-z\right|

small, with the smallest value being
\left|y-z\right|=d\mathfrak{r}
. But
then

\cos^{2}\tilde{\alpha}=1-\sin^{2}\tilde{\alpha}=1-\frac{\left(\left|z\right|\sin\alpha\right)^{2}}{\left|y-z\right|^{2}}

and hence the distance becomes

\left|i_{xyz}\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}\left(1+\frac{\left(\left|y\right|-\left|z\right|\cos\alpha\right)^{2}\left(\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}+\left|z\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha\right)}{\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}\left|z\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha}\right)+\left(\left|y\right|-\left|z\right|\cos\alpha\right)^{2}\right)\,.

We finally use
\left|y\right|=\left|z\right|\cos\alpha-\sqrt{\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}-\left|z\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha}

and obtain

\left|i_{xyz}\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}\left(1+\frac{\left(\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{4}-\left|z\right|^{4}\sin^{4}\alpha\right)}{\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}\left|z\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha}\right)+\left(\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}-\left|z\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha\right)\right)\,.

The latter expression now needs to be smaller than
d_{x}
. We observe
that the expression on the right hand side decreases for fixed
\alpha

if
\left|z\right|
increases.

On the other hand, we can resolve
\left|z\right|(y)=\left|y\right|\cos\alpha-\sqrt{\left|y\right|^{2}\sin^{2}\alpha+\left(d\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}}
.
From the conditions
\left|y\right|\leq d_{x}
and
\left|i_{xyz}\right|\leq d_{x}
,
we then infer (
6.2
).
∎

Theorem 6.5
.

Assuming
\mathbb{E}{\left(\delta^{-s-d}+\delta^{1+s-2d}\right)}^{\frac{p}{p-s}}<\infty

and using the notation of Lemma
5.2
the above constructed

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
has the property that for
1\leq r<s<p
there almost surely
exists
C>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
u\in W_{0,\partial(n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}^{1,p}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\left|n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{d_{j}^{-\tilde{\alpha}\frac{s}{r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{k}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}u\right|^{s}\right)^{\frac{r}{s}}+F_{s}^{3}(n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},u)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{\left|n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\nabla u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}\,,

and for every
u\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{W}}_{0,\partial(n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}^{1,p}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\left|n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\sum_{k:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{k}>0}\sum_{j:\,\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}<0}\frac{d_{j}^{-\tilde{\alpha}\frac{s}{r}}\left|\partial_{l}\Phi_{j}\right|}{D_{l+}^{\Phi}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{j}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right|^{s}\right)^{\frac{r}{s}}+F_{s}^{3,\mathfrak{s}}(n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},u)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{\left|n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap n\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\left|\nabla^{s}u\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{r}{p}}\,.

Lemma 6.6
.

For every bounded open set
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}

with
0\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
and
n_{0},n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
let

\forall M>1:\qquad\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{M,n_{0},n_{1}}:=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{n_{0}d_{a}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}\cap M\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathbb{B}_{n_{1}d_{a}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}\,.

Then for fixed
n_{0}
and
n_{1}
there almost surely exists
r>0

such that for every
M>1
it holds
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{M,n_{0},n_{1}}\subset Mr\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}

Proof.

There exists
r_{0}<R
such that
\mathbb{B}_{r_{0}}{\left(0\right)}\subset\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\subset\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}

we assume w.l.o.g
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}=\mathbb{B}_{R}{\left(0\right)}
. We denote
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}:=M\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
and
observe that
\frac{\left|\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\right|}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\right|}\leq C\,M^{-1}

where
\left|\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\right|:=\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M})
.
For

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}:=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}:\,a<\mathrm{dist}(x,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M})<b\right\}\,,

we observe that
\#{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}\right)}\leq CM^{d-1}(b-a)

due to the minimal mutual distance. The probability that at least
one
x\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}
satisfies
\mathbb{B}_{n_{0}d(x)}{\left(x\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\neq\emptyset

is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M},a,b\right)}:

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}{\left(\exists x\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}:\;\mathbb{B}_{n_{0}d(x)}{\left(x\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}\neq\emptyset\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k\,\mathbb{P}{\left(k=\#\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\cap\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}\right)}\,\mathbb{P}{\left(d>\frac{a}{n_{0}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}{\left(d>\frac{a}{n_{0}}\right)}e^{-\lambda\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\right|}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\right|^{k}}{\left(k-1\right)!}=\mathbb{P}{\left(d>\frac{a}{n_{0}}\right)}\lambda\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\right|\,.

Now let
r>0
and observe
\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M,a,b}\right|\leq C\,(b-a)\,\left(b+MR\right)^{d-1}

while
\mathbb{P}{\left(d>\frac{a}{n_{0}}\right)}\leq Ce^{-\alpha\,a^{d}}
. Then the
probability that there exists
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}\backslash\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{rM}
such
that
\mathbb{B}_{n_{0}d(x)}{\left(x\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M}
is smaller than

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle{\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{M},(r-1)M+k,(r-1)M+k+1\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}{\left(d>\frac{(r-1)M+k}{n_{0}}\right)}\lambda\left((rM+k+1)^{d}-(rM+k)^{d}\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha((r-1)M)^{d}}(rM)^{d}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha k^{d}}\lambda(k+2)^{d}\,,

and the right hand side tends uniformly to
0
as
r\to\infty
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.5
.

In what follows, we will mostly perform the calculations for
\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}

and
\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}
since these calculations are more involved and
drop
n
except for the last Step 4.

We first estimate the difference
\left|\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right|

for two directly neighbored points
x_{a}\sim x_{b}
of the Delaunay
grid. These are connected through a cylindric pipe

P_{\delta,a,b}=P(x_{a},x_{b},\delta(a,b)):=\mathrm{conv}{\left(\mathbb{B}_{\delta(a,b)}{\left(x_{a}\right)}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\delta(a,b)}{\left(x_{b}\right)}\right)}

with round ends and of thickness
\delta(a,b)
and total length
\left|x_{a}-x_{b}\right|+2\delta(a,b)<2\left|x_{a}-x_{b}\right|

and we first introduce the new averages in the spirit of (
2.27
)

\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\delta}u:=\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\delta}{\left(x_{a}\right)}}u\,,\qquad\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta}u(x):=\overline{\nabla_{a,\delta}^{\bot}}u{\left(x-a\right)}+\fint_{\mathbb{B}_{\delta}{\left(x_{a}\right)}}u\,.

As for (
4.15
) and (
4.23
)
we obtain

\mathcal{M}_{a_{1}}^{\delta_{1}}\mathcal{M}_{a_{2}}^{\delta_{2}}u=\mathcal{M}_{a_{2}}^{\delta_{2}}u\,,\qquad\mathcal{M}_{a_{1}}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta_{1}}\mathcal{M}_{a_{2}}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta_{2}}u=\mathcal{M}_{a_{2}}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta_{2}}u\,.

For every
i,a\in\mathbb{N}
with
p_{i}\in\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}
there exists
almost surely
a_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p_{i}
and
x_{a_{i}}
are
connected in
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}
through a straight line segment (i.e.
p_{i}

lies on the boundary of one of the pipes emerging at
x_{a_{i}}

or in
\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(x_{a_{i}}\right)}
) and

\displaystyle\left|\tau_{i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{s}\left(\left|\tau_{i}u-\mathcal{M}_{a_{i}}u\right|^{s}+\left|\mathcal{M}_{a_{i}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a}u\right|^{s}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\left|\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u(x)-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u(x)\right|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{s}\left(\left|\tau_{i}^{\mathfrak{s}}u(x)-\mathcal{M}_{a_{i}}^{\mathfrak{s}}u(x)\right|^{s}+\left|\mathcal{M}_{a_{i}}^{\mathfrak{s}}u(x)-\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u(x)\right|^{s}\right)\,.

The second term is of “mesoscopic type”, while the first term
is of local type. We will study both types of terms separately.

Step 1:
Using (
2.28
)–(
2.29
),
we observe for neighbors
a\sim b

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=a,b}\left|\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{k}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta(a,b)}u\right|^{s}+\left|\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta(a,b)}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}^{\mathfrak{s},\delta(a,b)}u\right|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq CF_{s}^{\mathfrak{s},1}{\left(x,\delta(a,b)\right)}\left(\left|x-x_{a}\right|^{s}+\left|x-x_{b}\right|^{s}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\left(\left\|\nabla^{s}u\right\|_{L^{s}(\mathbb{B}_{\frac{\mathfrak{r}}{16}}{\left(\left\{x_{a},x_{b}\right\}\right)})}^{s}+\left|x_{a}-x_{b}\right|^{2s}\left\|\nabla^{s}u\right\|_{L^{s}(P_{\delta,a,b})}^{s}\right)\,.

(6.3)

where

F_{s}^{\mathfrak{s},q}(x,\delta):=\left(\delta^{-d}+\delta^{-s-d}+\delta^{1+s-2d}\right)^{q}\,.

(6.4)

Step 2:
For reasons that we will encounter below, we define

\mathrm{I}_{\alpha}:=\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\sum_{a:\,\mathbb{B}_{4d_{a}}{\left(x_{a}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\neq\emptyset}\chi_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b:\,\mathbb{B}_{4d_{b}}{\left(x_{b}\right)}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\neq\emptyset\\
d_{b}\leq d_{a},\,\left|x_{a}-x_{b}\right|\leq 3d_{a}\end{subarray}}d_{a}^{-\alpha\frac{s}{r}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right|^{s}\,.

Assume
\chi_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\not\equiv 0
. Then
it holds
p_{i}\in\mathbb{B}_{2d_{a_{i}}}{\left(x_{a_{i}}\right)}
which implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\sum_{x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}f_{a}

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\partial_{l}\phi_{i}\right|}{D_{l+}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{a_{i}}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\right|}\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{P}}\cap\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\sum_{x_{a}\in\mathbb{X}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{b}\in\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{mat}}\\
\mathbb{B}_{2d_{b}}{\left(x_{b}\right)}\cap\mathbb{B}_{\mathfrak{r}}{\left(G_{a}\right)}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sum_{i:\,x_{a_{i}}=x_{b}}f_{a}\chi_{A_{1,i}}\left|\mathcal{M}_{a}^{\mathfrak{s}}u-\mathcal{M}_{b}^{\mathfrak{s}}u\right|^{s}
